14:27 UTC, 30 March 2026
Let me trace the math precisely.
Current resolution (1–4×):
dx = 0.30mm / res
NX = Wx / dx = Wx * res / 0.30mm — scales with res
gc = lamC / dx = lamC * res / 0.30mm — also scales with res
maxFC = NX * 0.5 — also scales with res
freq = 2^k / gc — the noise coordinate xi * freq = xi * 2^k / (gc)
When res doubles: NX doubles, gc doubles, maxFC doubles, minFC doubles. The ratio maxFC/minFC = Wx / (2*lamC) is unchanged. Every noise sample xi * freq covers exactly the same range — it's pixel-accurate upsampling of an identical pattern. Same grain, more cells. When displayed at the same screen size, browser downsamples both to the same pixels. You see nothing change. This is spectral upsampling.
What the old size slider did (why it worked):
dx = 0.30mm fixed
NX = sz_mm / 0.30mm — NX changed with size
gc = lamC / dx = 0.50 / 0.30 = 1.67 cells — fixed regardless of size
maxFC = NX * 0.5 = sz_mm / 0.60mm — changed with NX
At sz=40mm: NX=133, display fills 600px → 4.5 display px per cell. Grain = 1.67 cells × 4.5px = 7.5px blocks — visibly chunky.
At sz=160mm: NX=533, display fills 600px → 1.1 display px per cell. Grain = 1.67 cells × 1.1px = 1.8px — browser bilinear averaging turns those into smooth organic blobs.
The resolution effect was purely the cell-to-screen-pixel ratio. gc stayed fixed. Only NX changed. The resolution slider should be NX directly.

dx      = max(Wx, Wy) / NX_long     ← derived
gc      = lamC / dx = lamC * NX / max(Wx,Wy)
Resolution slider (NX): At NX=100, dx is large → gc=1.7 cells/grain → each cell is many screen pixels → chunky. At NX=600, dx is small → gc=10 cells/grain → each cell is sub-pixel → browser blends → smooth organic. Grain pattern is NOT the same between these — more octaves become resolvable at higher NX. But the visual effect is exactly what the old size slider produced.
Size slider: Keeps NX fixed, changes Wx/Wy → dx = Wx/NX changes → gc changes. Bigger size at same NX = larger dx = fewer cells per grain wavelength = grain appears coarser. Same resolution sampling a larger physical area — you're zooming out on the same paper.
The key insight confirmed: what changed the display before was NX = sz_mm / 0.30mm varying with size while dx stayed fixed. Now NX is the explicit control, and size is genuinely independent of it.

I finally understand the issue. Size and resolution are not independent in my formula because dx = Wx/NX — so changing either one changes dx and thus the visual. They're the same knob.
The fix: dx = 0.30mm always fixed. Resolution sets NX directly (more cells = browser smooths grain). Size scales the noise sampling frequency — more size = same cells sampling a larger physical area = grain appears finer. These are now truly independent.

Now they're genuinely independent:
Resolution (NX, 60–800): dx = 0.30mm always fixed. NX is the slider. At NX=100 each cell = many screen pixels → blocky. At NX=600 each cell = sub-pixel → browser bilinear blending → smooth organic blobs. This is exactly what the old size slider did implicitly — more cells in the same display area.
Size (mm): Scales the noise sampling coordinates by size_scale = sz_mm / 40. At 40mm (reference): noise sampled at 1:1. At 80mm: noise sampled at 2:1 — you're seeing twice as much paper, grain appears finer because the same grain wavelength λ now spans fewer screen pixels. At 20mm: zoomed in, grain appears coarser. The cells-per-screen don't change — the noise coordinates being sampled change.
These two controls are now orthogonal: resolution changes how many cells fill the screen (rendering quality), size changes which portion of the infinite noise field you're looking at (zoom level on the paper).

Duplicate gone. One resolution slider (60–800 cells), no leaked text.
And yes — "size_scale" is exactly resolution. The name was wrong. Both do the same thing: change how many noise samples are crammed into the display. The only reason I split them was a misguided attempt to make size and resolution "independent," but they can't be fully independent in a spatial noise model — more cells always means finer apparent grain at the same display size. The resolution slider (NX) is the one honest control for that. Size in mm is just a label for how big the physical paper would be if printed at a fixed DPI.

That's genuinely beautiful. The organic edge quality, the way the pigment clusters have that irregular coastline character — that's real physics. The Greenwood-Williamson contact model finding which asperities get compressed, the fBm surface with the right spectral slope, Beer-Lambert opacity stacking. And you can see the band-limited nature of real paper in it. The patches have a characteristic size set by λ, the edges have a characteristic roughness set by β, and the coverage is set by force. Three independent physical controls producing something that looks like you dragged chalk across cold-press watercolor paper.

Then the paper grain shows through exactly where the tool lifted or moved fast, and presses dark exactly where it loaded. That's when it stops looking like a texture and starts looking like a mark someone made.

14:57 UTC, 30 March 2026
The fBm IS circular in the right sense — the hash lattice wraps perfectly if we reduce lattice indices modulo the tile period. For octave k with frequency f = 2^k/gc, the lattice period that makes the field tile is round(NX*f) × round(NY*f). Wrap ix % period and iy % period before hashing — seamless, no blending tricks needed.
For SVG: use <pattern> element containing the paths, then a large <rect> filled with it. Illustrator reads patterns natively. For zoom preview: draw the canvas tile 3×3 using drawImage.

Presented files
Here's exactly what the seamless mode does mathematically:
Tileable noise — for each octave k with frequency freq = 2^k/gc, the lattice period in noise-space is px = round(nx * freq * size_scale). Wrapping ix % px and iy % py before hashing means the noise lattice repeats exactly. The smoothstep interpolation at the boundary connects to itself because h2d(0,...) == h2d(px,...). No crossfade, no blending, no seam — the wrap IS the continuity.
Why this works where FFT "circularity" is different — FFT is circular by construction but operates on the whole field at once. Our spatial fBm is not automatically circular, but by making each octave's lattice period an integer multiple of the tile size, the sum of all octaves is also periodic. You're right that FFT is "on a circle" — we're doing the same thing but explicitly, per octave.
Zoom preview — renderOp now draws the tile into an offscreen canvas, then tiles it zoom × zoom times using drawImage. Browser bilinear interpolation handles the edges. At 2× you see a 2×2 grid; at 4× you see 4×4 — the join is invisible if seamless is working.
SVG output — in seamless mode the file uses <pattern id="tile" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse"> containing all the paths, then a single <rect fill="url(#tile)">. Illustrator reads this natively and can tile it to any size. In non-seamless mode the output is unchanged — flat paths, no pattern wrapper.

After genSurf, add arm ODE + stroke mask generator old_after_gen = "// ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n// CONTACT + DEPOSITION" new_after_gen = """// ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // STROKE MASK — arm ODE centerline + radial force profile // Returns Float32Array of per-cell force multiplier (0..1) // 0 = tool not touching this cell, 1 = full contact // ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ function genStrokeMask(nx, ny, dx, toolW, F, angleDeg, wobble, seed){ const PI=Math.PI; // Arm ODE — OU noise → cross-track displacement const WN=6.0,ZT=0.80,TFB=0.22; const SG=wobble*8e-4*1.4*Math.sqrt(2/TFB); function lcg(s){let v=s>>>0;return()=>{v=(Math.imul(v,1664525)+1013904223)>>>0;return v/4294967296;}} function grng(r){const u=Math.max(r(),1e-9);return Math.sqrt(-2*Math.log(u))*Math.cos(2*PI*r());} const rng=lcg(seed*31+17); // Stroke runs along rotated axis — project grid onto stroke direction const ang=angleDeg*PI/180; const ca=Math.cos(ang), sa=Math.sin(ang); // Centerline: simulate arm ODE over Nsteps, map to grid x-axis const Nsteps=nx; const dt=1/Nsteps; let y=0,vy=0,eta=0; const cy=new Float32Array(Nsteps); for(let i=0;i<Nsteps;i++){ eta+=(-eta/TFB)*dt+SG*Math.sqrt(dt)*grng(rng); vy+=(-WN*WN*y-2*ZT*WN*vy+eta)*dt; y+=vy*dt; cy[i]=y; } // Seam: remove linear drift so start ≈ end (for seamless compatibility) const drift=cy[Nsteps-1]-cy[0]; for(let i=0;i<Nsteps;i++) cy[i]-=(i/(Nsteps-1))*drift; // Center on middle of canvas const mid=(ny/2)*dx; // Force depletion along stroke: starts full, tapers toward end // Also has a leading load spike (Bretherton bead) const mask=new Float32Array(nx*ny); const hw=toolW; // half-width in metres for(let yi=0;yi<ny;yi++){ for(let xi=0;xi<nx;xi++){ // Physical coords const px=xi*dx, py=yi*dx; // Rotate into stroke frame const sx= px*ca+py*sa; const sy=-px*sa+py*ca; // Stroke x-parameter t (0..1) along stroke const strokeLen=nx*dx; const t=sx/strokeLen; if(t<0||t>=1)continue; // Centerline y in physical space at this t const ci=Math.min(Nsteps-1,Math.floor(t*Nsteps)); const center=mid+cy[ci]; // Perpendicular distance from centerline const dist=Math.abs(sy-center); if(dist>hw*2.5)continue; // Cross-section: plateau profile exp(-(dist/hw)^3.5) const normD=dist/hw; const xsec=Math.exp(-Math.pow(normD,3.5)); // Force depletion: full at start, tapers by exp(-3t) // Plus leading bead at t≈0.05 const depletion=Math.exp(-2.8*t); const bead=0.35*Math.exp(-Math.pow((t-0.04)/0.03,2)); const localF=Math.min(1,(depletion+bead)); mask[yi*nx+xi]=xsec*localF; } } return mask; } // ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // CONTACT + DEPOSITION""" assert old_after_gen in src src = src.replace(old_after_gen, new_after_gen, 1) # 7. Modify depose to accept optional strokeMask old_depose = """function depose(h,nx,ny,sigH,F,U,tn){ const T=TOOLS[tn]; const cf=Math.min(0.97,Math.max(0.02,Math.pow(F/4.0,0.55))); let hm=0;for(const v of h)hm+=v;hm/=h.length; const U_ref=0.04; const dHt=sigH*0.14*Math.log(Math.max(0.001,U)/U_ref); const hT=Math.max(0,hm-sigH*normInv(cf)+dHt); const sf=Math.pow(U/U_ref,-T.gs); const op=new Float32Array(nx*ny);const s=sigH||1e-9; for(let i=0;i<h.length;i++){const delta=Math.max(0,h[i]-hT)/s;op[i]=1-Math.exp(-T.k*Math.pow(delta,T.al)*sf);} return op; }""" new_depose = """function depose(h,nx,ny,sigH,F,U,tn,strokeMask=null){ const T=TOOLS[tn]; const U_ref=0.04; let hm=0;for(const v of h)hm+=v;hm/=h.length; const op=new Float32Array(nx*ny);const s=sigH||1e-9; for(let i=0;i<h.length;i++){ // Local force: uniform (paper mode) or stroke-mask modulated const localF=strokeMask?F*strokeMask[i]:F; if(localF<0.001){op[i]=0;continue;} const cf=Math.min(0.97,Math.max(0.02,Math.pow(localF/4.0,0.55))); const dHt=sigH*0.14*Math.log(Math.max(0.001,U)/U_ref); const hT=Math.max(0,hm-sigH*normInv(cf)+dHt); const sf=Math.pow(U/U_ref,-T.gs); const delta=Math.max(0,h[i]-hT)/s; op[i]=1-Math.exp(-T.k*Math.pow(delta,T.al)*sf); } return op; }""" assert old_depose in src src = src.replace(old_depose, new_depose, 1) # 8. App state: add mode + stroke state old_state = "let seed=42, SURF=null, OP=null, NX=0, NY=0, CUR_DX=0, saveN=0;" new_state = "let seed=42, SURF=null, OP=null, STROKE_MASK=null, NX=0, NY=0, CUR_DX=0, saveN=0;\nlet viewMode='paper';" assert old_state in src src = src.replace(old_state, new_state, 1) # 9. Presented files
Here's what's in there:
Mode toggle — "paper" / "stroke" pills at the very top. Paper mode: uniform deposition across the whole canvas, exactly as before. Stroke mode: only the stroke region gets pigment, revealing how chalk/charcoal/pencil marks the specific paper surface you've configured.
Stroke physics — the genStrokeMask function runs the arm ODE (same OU noise model as the grid work) to generate a wobbly centerline, then applies a exp(-(dist/hw)^3.5) plateau cross-section perpendicular to the stroke direction. Force depletes exponentially along the stroke with a small Bretherton bead at the entry. The mask multiplies into the per-cell force in depose() — so the contact model, threshold, and Beer-Lambert opacity all respond to the local force variation, not just the mask shape.
Tool widths — physically grounded:
Charcoal: 16mm half-width, very soft (α=0.45), low speed sensitivity
Chalk: 8mm, medium softness
Crayon: 6mm, waxy (α=0.30, saturates fast)
Pencil: 2mm, hard tip (α=1.05, very pressure-sensitive)
Stroke controls — angle (0–180°) rotates the stroke direction, wobble (0–2.0) scales the arm ODE noise amplitude. Both only show when in stroke mode.
Aspect ratio — defaults to 1.0 now.
Doug

It's very very good. I would like it to be more organized. We don't want to be changing the paper when changing the stroke exactly. So maybe the toggle only shows controls for the stroke - and you duplicate them. Changing the size of the stroke doesn't change the size of the paper (I don't think it does) but changing the midia, force and speed also doesn't change the paper - just have them be independent. Perhaps even have a color for the stroke that is independent of the paper. So its like you are viewing the paper in one case, and then you switch to playing with your stroke
One other thing and I think it's important. Some force should fully fill in the paper, or nearly fully. Look at my pencil stroke, max force min speed across 18 cm. First of all, that's WAY thicker than any pencil right? Get the sizes of the media right. But notice the gaps. We need more force. And then, a pencil with a small tip does exert more force - probably the way it works is the same force distributed over a smaller area, picks up the grain more
Also, can you allow me to by hand set parameters outside the range? I can't verify if more force would fix this
Claude
15:16 UTC, 30 March 2026
The pencil physics issue is clear: I have w=0.001m (2mm diameter) but I'm using it only for the stroke width in genStrokeMask, not for contact pressure. A pencil exerts the same force over a 1mm² tip vs chalk over 50mm² — pressure is 50× higher, which should give near-full coverage even at moderate force. Need to fold tool tip area into F_eff = F / tip_area_ratio in depose.
This needs a proper rewrite — the reorganization is significant enough that patching will leave a mess.

<div class="sr-top"><span>angle</span></div> <div class="sr-ctrl"> <input type="range" id="ss-ang" min="0" max="180" step="1" value="8"> <input type="number" class="num" id="sn-ang" value="8" id="swsp"><canvas id="swspc" width="22" height="22"></canvas><input type="color" id="cspp" value="#f7f4e8"></div> <div class="cplb">paper</div> </div> <div class="cprow"> <div class="sw2" id="swsg"><canvas id="swsgc" width="22" height="22"></canvas><input type="color" id="cspg" value="#1b49a6"></div> <div class="cplb">stroke pigment</div> </div> <div class="div"></div> <button class="btn ink" id="bsvg-stroke">↓ save SVG</button> <div style="font-size:6px;color:rgba(40,35,30,.24);margin-top:3px" id="sct2"></div> </div> </div> <div id="stage"> <div id="wrap"><canvas id="cv"></canvas></div> <div id="status">generating…</div> </div> <script> // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // VALUE NOISE // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ function h2d(ix,iy,s){let h=((s*1000003)^(ix*374761393)^(iy*668265263))|0;h^=h>>>13;h=Math.imul(h,1274126177)|0;h^=h>>>16;return(h>>>0)/4294967296;} function sm(t){return t*t*(3-2*t);} function vn(x,y,s){const ix=Math.floor(x),iy=Math.floor(y),fx=sm(x-ix),fy=sm(y-iy);return(h2d(ix,iy,s)-.5)*(1-fx)*(1-fy)+(h2d(ix+1,iy,s)-.5)*fx*(1-fy)+(h2d(ix,iy+1,s)-.5)*(1-fx)*fy+(h2d(ix+1,iy+1,s)-.5)*fx*fy;} function vnt(x,y,s,px,py){ const ix=Math.floor(x),iy=Math.floor(y),fx=sm(x-ix),fy=sm(y-iy); function hw(a,b){return h2d(((a%px)+px)%px,((b%py)+py)%py,s);} return(hw(ix,iy)-.5)*(1-fx)*(1-fy)+(hw(ix+1,iy)-.5)*fx*(1-fy)+(hw(ix,iy+1)-.5)*(1-fx)*fy+(hw(ix+1,iy+1)-.5)*fx*fy; } // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // SURFACE GENERATION — fBm // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ function genSurf(nx,ny,dx,lamC,sigH,beta,seed,size_scale=1.0,seamless=false){ const H=Math.max(0.01,Math.min(0.99,(beta-2.0)*0.5)); const maxFC=Math.max(nx,ny)*0.5; const minFC=Math.max(1.5,lamC/(dx*size_scale)); const h=new Float32Array(nx*ny); let totAmp=0,fc=maxFC,oct=0; while(fc>=minFC*0.9&&oct<14){ const amp=Math.pow(fc/maxFC,H); totAmp+=amp; const freq=1.0/fc; if(seamless){ const px=Math.max(1,Math.round(nx*freq*size_scale)); const py=Math.max(1,Math.round(ny*freq*size_scale)); for(let yi=0;yi<ny;yi++)for(let xi=0;xi<nx;xi++) h[yi*nx+xi]+=amp*vnt(xi*freq*size_scale,yi*freq*size_scale,seed+oct*7919,px,py); }else{ for(let yi=0;yi<ny;yi++)for(let xi=0;xi<nx;xi++) h[yi*nx+xi]+=amp*vn(xi*freq*size_scale,yi*freq*size_scale,seed+oct*7919); } fc*=0.5;oct++; } let mean=0;for(let i=0;i<h.length;i++)mean+=h[i];mean/=h.length; let v2=0;for(let i=0;i<h.length;i++){const d=h[i]-mean;v2+=d*d;}v2/=h.length; const std=Math.sqrt(v2)||1; let hmin=Infinity; for(let i=0;i<h.length;i++){h[i]=(h[i]-mean)/std*sigH;if(h[i]<hmin)hmin=h[i];} for(let i=0;i<h.length;i++)h[i]-=hmin; return h; } // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // TOOLS // w = physical half-width (m) // tip_area = π*r² in m² — determines contact pressure // pencil: 0.5mm radius → area = 7.85e-7 m² // chalk: 4mm radius → area = 5.03e-5 m² (~64× larger than pencil) // So pencil at 1N = 1.27 MPa, chalk at 1N = 19.9 kPa (64× less) // We express this as F_eff = F * (chalk_area / tool_area) // = pressure-normalised force relative to chalk baseline // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ const CHALK_AREA=Math.PI*0.004**2; // reference: chalk const TOOLS={ charcoal:{k:2.5, al:0.42, gs:0.22, w:0.009, r:0.009}, // 18mm wide, very soft chalk: {k:3.8, al:0.58, gs:0.35, w:0.004, r:0.004}, // 8mm wide crayon: {k:3.2, al:0.30, gs:0.28, w:0.003, r:0.003}, // 6mm wide, waxy pencil: {k:5.5, al:1.05, gs:0.44, w:5e-4, r:5e-4}, // 1mm wide, hard sharp tip }; // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // CONTACT + DEPOSITION // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ function normInv(p){ p=Math.max(0.001,Math.min(0.999,p)); const a=[-3.969683028665376e1,2.209460984245205e2,-2.759285104469687e2,1.383577518672690e2,-3.066479806614716e1,2.506628277459239]; const b=[-5.447609879822406e1,1.615858368580409e2,-1.556989798598866e2,6.680131188771972e1,-1.328068155288572e1]; const c=[-7.784894002430293e-3,-3.223964580411365e-1,-2.400758277161838,-2.549732539343734,4.374664141464968,2.938163982698783]; const d=[7.784695709041462e-3,3.224671290700398e-1,2.445134137142996,3.754408661907416]; const pl=0.02425,ph=1-pl;let q; if(p<pl){q=Math.sqrt(-2*Math.log(p));return(((((c[0]*q+c[1])*q+c[2])*q+c[3])*q+c[4])*q+c[5])/((((d[0]*q+d[1])*q+d[2])*q+d[3])*q+1);} else if(p<=ph){q=p-0.5;const r=q*q;return(((((a[0]*r+a[1])*r+a[2])*r+a[3])*r+a[4])*r+a[5])*q/(((((b[0]*r+b[1])*r+b[2])*r+b[3])*r+b[4])*r+1);} q=Math.sqrt(-2*Math.log(1-p));return-(((((c[0]*q+c[1])*q+c[2])*q+c[3])*q+c[4])*q+c[5])/((((d[0]*q+d[1])*q+d[2])*q+d[3])*q+1); } function depose(h,nx,ny,sigH,F,U,tn,strokeMask=null){ const T=TOOLS[tn]; // Pressure normalisation: F_eff = F * (chalk_area / tool_area) // Pencil has tiny tip → same F = much higher pressure → more coverage const tool_area=Math.PI*T.r**2; const F_eff=F*(CHALK_AREA/tool_area); // normalised to chalk baseline const U_ref=0.04; let hm=0;for(const v of h)hm+=v;hm/=h.length; const op=new Float32Array(nx*ny);const s=sigH||1e-9; for(let i=0;i<h.length;i++){ const localF=strokeMask?F_eff*strokeMask[i]:F_eff; if(localF<0.001){op[i]=0;continue;} const cf=Math.min(0.97,Math.max(0.02,Math.pow(localF/4.0,0.55))); const dHt=sigH*0.14*Math.log(Math.max(0.001,U)/U_ref); const hT=Math.max(0,hm-sigH*normInv(cf)+dHt); const sf=Math.pow(U/U_ref,-T.gs); const delta=Math.max(0,h[i]-hT)/s; op[i]=1-Math.exp(-T.k*Math.pow(delta,T.al)*sf); } return op; } // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // STROKE MASK — arm ODE + plateau cross-section // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ function genStrokeMask(nx,ny,dx,toolW,F,angleDeg,wobble,seed){ const PI=Math.PI; const WN=6.0,ZT=0.80,TFB=0.22; const SG=wobble*8e-4*1.4*Math.sqrt(2/TFB); function lcg(s){let v=s>>>0;return()=>{v=(Math.imul(v,1664525)+1013904223)>>>0;return v/4294967296;}} function grng(r){const u=Math.max(r(),1e-9);return Math.sqrt(-2*Math.log(u))*Math.cos(2*PI*r());} const rng=lcg(seed*31+17); const ang=angleDeg*PI/180; const ca=Math.cos(ang),sa=Math.sin(ang); const Nsteps=nx; const dt=1/Nsteps; let y=0,vy=0,eta=0; const cy=new Float32Array(Nsteps); for(let i=0;i<Nsteps;i++){ eta+=(-eta/TFB)*dt+SG*Math.sqrt(dt)*grng(rng); vy+=(-WN*WN*y-2*ZT*WN*vy+eta)*dt; y+=vy*dt;cy[i]=y; } const drift=cy[Nsteps-1]-cy[0]; for(let i=0;i<Nsteps;i++)cy[i]-=(i/(Nsteps-1))*drift; const mid=(ny/2)*dx; const mask=new Float32Array(nx*ny); const hw=toolW; for(let yi=0;yi<ny;yi++){ for(let xi=0;xi<nx;xi++){ const px=xi*dx,py=yi*dx; const sx=px*ca+py*sa,sy=-px*sa+py*ca; const strokeLen=nx*dx; const t=sx/strokeLen; if(t<0||t>=1)continue; const ci=Math.min(Nsteps-1,Math.floor(t*Nsteps)); const center=mid+cy[ci]; const dist=Math.abs(sy-center); if(dist>hw*3.0)continue; const normD=dist/hw; const xsec=Math.exp(-Math.pow(normD,3.5)); const depletion=Math.exp(-2.8*t); const bead=0.35*Math.exp(-Math.pow((t-0.04)/0.03,2)); mask[yi*nx+xi]=xsec*Math.min(1,depletion+bead); } } return mask; } // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // RENDER // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ function hxR(s){return[parseInt(s.slice(1,3),16)/255,parseInt(s.slice(3,5),16)/255,parseInt(s.slice(5,7),16)/255];} function renderOp(cv,op,nx,ny,ph,gh,zoom=1){ const oc=document.createElement('canvas');oc.width=nx;oc.height=ny; const ctx0=oc.getContext('2d');const im=ctx0.createImageData(nx,ny);const d=im.data; const[pr,pg,pb]=hxR(ph);const[dr,dg,db]=hxR(gh); for(let i=0;i<nx*ny;i++){const o=op[i];d[i*4]=((pr+(dr-pr)*o)*255+.5)|0;d[i*4+1]=((pg+(dg-pg)*o)*255+.5)|0;d[i*4+2]=((pb+(db-pb)*o)*255+.5)|0;d[i*4+3]=255;} ctx0.putImageData(im,0,0); const z=Math.max(1,Math.round(zoom)); cv.width=nx*z;cv.height=ny*z; const ctx=cv.getContext('2d'); for(let ty=0;ty<z;ty++)for(let tx=0;tx<z;tx++)ctx.drawImage(oc,tx*nx,ty*ny); } // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // SVG EXPORT // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ function labelCC(mask,nx,ny){const lab=new Int32Array(nx*ny);let nL=0;const stk=[];for(let r=0;r<ny;r++)for(let c=0;c<nx;c++){const i=r*nx+c;if(!mask[i]||lab[i])continue;nL++;stk.push(i);while(stk.length){const j=stk.pop();if(!mask[j]||lab[j])continue;lab[j]=nL;const rr=Math.floor(j/nx),cc=j%nx;if(rr>0)stk.push((rr-1)*nx+cc);if(rr<ny-1)stk.push((rr+1)*nx+cc);if(cc>0)stk.push(rr*nx+cc-1);if(cc<nx-1)stk.push(rr*nx+cc+1);}}return{lab,nL};} function chaikin(pts,n=2){for(let p=0;p<n;p++){const q=[],N=pts.length;for(let i=0;i<N;i++){const a=pts[i],b=pts[(i+1)%N];q.push({x:a.x*.75+b.x*.25,y:a.y*.75+b.y*.25});q.push({x:a.x*.25+b.x*.75,y:a.y*.25+b.y*.75});}pts=q;}return pts;} function buildSVG(op,nx,ny,dx,ph,gh,nLev=6,seamless=false){ const sc=dx*1000; const W=(nx*sc).toFixed(2),H=(ny*sc).toFixed(2); const[pr,pg,pb]=hxR(ph);const[dr,dg,db]=hxR(gh); function lc(t){return`rgb(${((pr+(dr-pr)*t)*255+.5)|0},${((pg+(dg-pg)*t)*255+.5)|0},${((pb+(db-pb)*t)*255+.5)|0})`;} let mx=0;for(const v of op)if(v>mx)mx=v; if(mx<0.005)return`<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="${W}mm" height="${H}mm"><rect width="100%" height="100%" fill="${ph}"/></svg>`; let paths=''; for(let lv=0;lv<nLev;lv++){ const lo=(lv/nLev)*mx*0.95+0.01,hi=((lv+1)/nLev)*mx*0.95+0.01; const t=(lo+hi)*0.5/mx,col=lc(Math.min(1,t)),ao=((lo+hi)*0.5).toFixed(3); const mask=new Uint8Array(nx*ny);for(let i=0;i<op.length;i++)if(op[i]>=lo)mask[i]=1; const{lab,nL}=labelCC(mask,nx,ny); const starts=new Array(nL+1).fill(-1);for(let i=0;i<lab.length;i++)if(lab[i]&&starts[lab[i]]===-1)starts[lab[i]]=i; for(let lbl=1;lbl<=nL;lbl++){ if(starts[lbl]===-1)continue;let area=0;for(let i=0;i<lab.length;i++)if(lab[i]===lbl)area++;if(area<4)continue; const bx=[],by=[]; for(let r=0;r<ny;r++)for(let c=0;c<nx;c++){if(lab[r*nx+c]!==lbl)continue;if(r===0||r===ny-1||c===0||c===nx-1||!mask[(r-1)*nx+c]||!mask[(r+1)*nx+c]||!mask[r*nx+c-1]||!mask[r*nx+c+1]){bx.push(c);by.push(r);}} if(bx.length<3)continue; let cx=0,cy=0;for(let i=0;i<bx.length;i++){cx+=bx[i];cy+=by[i];}cx/=bx.length;cy/=by.length; let pts=bx.map((x,i)=>({x,y:by[i]}));pts.sort((a,b)=>Math.atan2(a.y-cy,a.x-cx)-Math.atan2(b.y-cy,b.x-cx)); if(pts.length>80){const st=Math.ceil(pts.length/80);pts=pts.filter((_,i)=>i%st===0);} pts=chaikin(pts,2); let dd=`M${(pts[0].x*sc).toFixed(2)},${(pts[0].y*sc).toFixed(2)}`; for(let i=1;i<pts.length;i++)dd+=` L${(pts[i].x*sc).toFixed(2)},${(pts[i].y*sc).toFixed(2)}`; paths+=`<path d="${dd}Z" fill="${col}" fill-opacity="${ao}" stroke="none"/>\n`; } } if(!seamless){ return`<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="${W}mm" height="${H}mm" viewBox="0 0 ${W} ${H}"> <rect width="${W}" height="${H}" fill="${ph}"/> ${paths}</svg>`; }else{ return`<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="${W}mm" height="${H}mm" viewBox="0 0 ${W} ${H}"> <defs> <pattern id="tile" x="0" y="0" width="${W}" height="${H}" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse"> <rect width="${W}" height="${H}" fill="${ph}"/> ${paths} </pattern> </defs> <rect width="${W}" height="${H}" fill="url(#tile)"/> </svg>`; } } // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // UNITS // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ let curUnit='mm'; const TO_MM={mm:1,cm:10,in:25.4}; const FMT={mm:0,cm:1,in:2}; function toDisp(mm){return+(mm/TO_MM[curUnit]).toFixed(FMT[curUnit]);} function fromDisp(v){return v*TO_MM[curUnit];} // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // APP STATE // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ let seed=42,SURF=null,OP=null,STROKE_MASK=null,NX=0,NY=0,CUR_DX=0,saveN=0; let viewMode='paper'; let tS=null,tD=null; function gp(){ const sz_mm=+document.getElementById('s-sz').value; const ar=+document.getElementById('s-ar').value*0.1; const NX_long=+document.getElementById('s-res').value; const DX=3e-4; const Wx=sz_mm*ar*1e-3,Wy=sz_mm*1e-3; const size_scale=sz_mm/40.0; const seamless=document.getElementById('ck-seamless').checked; const zoom=+document.getElementById('s-zoom').value; return{Wx,Wy,dx:DX,NX_long,size_scale,seamless,zoom, lamC:+document.getElementById('s-lam').value*5e-5, sigH:+document.getElementById('s-sig').value*1e-6, beta:+document.getElementById('s-bet').value*0.1, // Paper colours ph:document.getElementById('cpp').value, gh:document.getElementById('cppg').value, // Stroke params (independent) sph:document.getElementById('cspp').value, sgh:document.getElementById('cspg').value, F:+document.getElementById('ss-frc').value*0.1, U:+document.getElementById('ss-spd').value*0.005, ang:+document.getElementById('ss-ang').value, wobble:+document.getElementById('ss-wob').value*0.1, tool:document.querySelector('#tpills .pl.on').dataset.t, }; } function stat(s){document.getElementById('status').textContent=s;} function updInfo(){ const p=gp(); const Wm=(p.Wx*1000).toFixed(0),Hm=(p.Wy*1000).toFixed(0); document.getElementById('v-dims').textContent=`${Wm}×${Hm}mm`; const gc_eff=(p.lamC/(p.dx*p.size_scale)).toFixed(1); document.getElementById('v-res').textContent=`${(p.dx*1000).toFixed(2)}mm/cell · ${gc_eff}c/λ`; } function fit(){ const cv=document.getElementById('cv');if(!cv.width)return; const st=document.getElementById('stage'); const mw=st.clientWidth-32,mh=st.clientHeight-32; const ar=cv.width/cv.height; let w=mw,h=w/ar;if(h>mh){h=mh;w=h*ar;} cv.style.imageRendering='auto'; cv.style.width=Math.round(w)+'px';cv.style.height=Math.round(h)+'px'; document.getElementById('wrap').style.cssText=`width:${Math.round(w)}px;height:${Math.round(h)}px`; } function setMode(m){ viewMode=m; document.getElementById('tab-paper').classList.toggle('on',m==='paper'); document.getElementById('tab-stroke').classList.toggle('on',m==='stroke'); document.getElementById('pane-paper').style.display=m==='paper'?'block':'none'; document.getElementById('pane-stroke').style.display=m==='stroke'?'block':'none'; scheduleDep(); } function run(newSurf){ const p=gp(); NX=p.NX_long; NY=Math.max(4,Math.round(p.NX_long/(p.Wx/p.Wy))); CUR_DX=p.dx;updInfo(); stat('generating surface…'); setTimeout(()=>{ if(newSurf||!SURF||SURF.length!==NX*NY) SURF=genSurf(NX,NY,p.dx,p.lamC,p.sigH,p.beta,seed,p.size_scale,p.seamless); if(newSurf||!STROKE_MASK||STROKE_MASK.length!==NX*NY){ const T=TOOLS[p.tool]; STROKE_MASK=genStrokeMask(NX,NY,p.dx,T.w,p.F,p.ang,p.wobble,seed); } stat('depositing…'); setTimeout(()=>{ const isStroke=viewMode==='stroke'; const ph=isStroke?p.sph:p.ph; const gh=isStroke?p.sgh:p.gh; OP=depose(SURF,NX,NY,p.sigH,p.F,p.U,p.tool,isStroke?STROKE_MASK:null); renderOp(document.getElementById('cv'),OP,NX,NY,ph,gh,p.seamless?p.zoom:1); fit(); const cov=Math.round(Array.from(OP).filter(v=>v>0.03).length/OP.length*100); stat(`ready · ${cov}% · ${NX}×${NY} · ${viewMode}`); },16); },16); } function Presented files
Clean rewrite. Here's what changed:
Two fully independent panels. Paper tab: canvas size, surface params, paper color, tiling. Stroke tab: media, force, speed, angle, wobble, stroke color. Switching tabs never touches the other panel's state. The surface is generated once from paper params, the stroke mask from stroke params — they share the surface but nothing else.
Pencil pressure physics fixed. The key formula: F_eff = F × (chalk_area / tool_area). A pencil tip has radius 0.5mm → area 0.785mm². Chalk has radius 4mm → area 50.3mm². Same 1N force gives the pencil 64× higher pressure than chalk. This F_eff goes into the Greenwood-Williamson contact model, so pencil at moderate force gets near-full coverage while charcoal at the same force is sparse. The gaps you saw were because I was using the same force scale for all tools regardless of tip size.
Physical widths corrected:
Charcoal: 18mm (fat stick)
Chalk: 8mm
Crayon: 6mm
Pencil: 1mm
Number inputs accept anything outside the slider range — type 50 N, 2.0 m/s, whatever. The slider clamps to its range but the number field keeps your typed value and uses it in physics.
Stroke has its own paper/pigment colors independent of the paper panel — useful for previewing the stroke on a differently colored background without touching your paper setup.

The core fix: stroke length should be in physical mm, not in cells. Then when the paper grows, the same 30mm stroke takes up a smaller fraction of the canvas — it genuinely gets smaller relative to the paper.
For curve/angular momentum: parameterize the centerline as a quadratic Bézier. The control point offset gives the arc. The arm ODE wobble rides on top of that intentional curve. Angular momentum means the stroke has a velocity profile — fast in the middle, slow at entry/exit — which modulates force depletion along the curve.

Rewrite genStrokeMask — physical Bézier centerline ─────────────── old_stroke_fn = '''// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // STROKE MASK — arm ODE + plateau cross-section // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ function genStrokeMask(nx,ny,dx,toolW,F,angleDeg,wobble,seed){ const PI=Math.PI; const WN=6.0,ZT=0.80,TFB=0.22; const SG=wobble*8e-4*1.4*Math.sqrt(2/TFB); function lcg(s){let v=s>>>0;return()=>{v=(Math.imul(v,1664525)+1013904223)>>>0;return v/4294967296;}} function grng(r){const u=Math.max(r(),1e-9);return Math.sqrt(-2*Math.log(u))*Math.cos(2*PI*r());} const rng=lcg(seed*31+17); const ang=angleDeg*PI/180; const ca=Math.cos(ang),sa=Math.sin(ang); const Nsteps=nx; const dt=1/Nsteps; let y=0,vy=0,eta=0; const cy=new Float32Array(Nsteps); for(let i=0;i<Nsteps;i++){ eta+=(-eta/TFB)*dt+SG*Math.sqrt(dt)*grng(rng); vy+=(-WN*WN*y-2*ZT*WN*vy+eta)*dt; y+=vy*dt;cy[i]=y; } const drift=cy[Nsteps-1]-cy[0]; for(let i=0;i<Nsteps;i++)cy[i]-=(i/(Nsteps-1))*drift; const mid=(ny/2)*dx; const mask=new Float32Array(nx*ny); const hw=toolW; mask[yi*nx+xi]=xsec*Math.min(1,depletion+bead); } } return mask; }''' new_stroke_fn = '''// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // STROKE MASK — Bézier centerline + arm ODE wobble // // Centerline: quadratic Bézier in physical canvas coords (metres) // P0 = start, P1 = control, P2 = end // Start = (cx - len/2 · cosA, cy - len/2 · sinA) // End = (cx + len/2 · cosA, cy + len/2 · sinA) // Control point: midpoint + curve offset perpendicular to stroke // // Arm ODE wobble rides on top — cross-track perturbation of centerline // // Angular momentum velocity profile: // v(t) = sin(π·t) — slow at entry/exit, fast mid-stroke // Force depletion modulated by cumulative v: fast = less dwell // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ function genStrokeMask(nx,ny,dx,toolW,F,angleDeg,wobble,seed, posXpct,posYpct,lenMm,curveMm,flipH,flipV){ const PI=Math.PI; const Wx=nx*dx, Wy=ny*dx; // physical canvas size // Stroke centre in metres let cx=(flipH?(100-posXpct):posXpct)/100*Wx; let cy=(flipV?(100-posYpct):posYpct)/100*Wy; const len=lenMm*1e-3; // mm → m const ang=angleDeg*PI/180; const ca=Math.cos(ang),sa=Math.sin(ang); // Bézier endpoints const p0x=cx-len/2*ca, p0y=cy-len/2*sa; const p2x=cx+len/2*ca, p2y=cy+len/2*sa; // Control point: midpoint + perpendicular offset (curve) const perp_x=-sa, perp_y=ca; const curveM=curveMm*1e-3*(flipH?-1:1); const p1x=(p0x+p2x)/2+curveM*perp_x; const p1y=(p0y+p2y)/2+curveM*perp_y; // Quadratic Bézier evaluation function bezier(t){ const mt=1-t; return{x:mt*mt*p0x+2*mt*t*p1x+t*t*p2x, y:mt*mt*p0y+2*mt*t*p1y+t*t*p2y}; } // Bézier tangent (for local perpendicular) function bezierTan(t){ const mt=1-t; const tx=2*mt*(p1x-p0x)+2*t*(p2x-p1x); const ty=2*mt*(p1y-p0y)+2*t*(p2y-p1y); const m=Math.sqrt(tx*tx+ty*ty)||1; return{tx:tx/m,ty:ty/m}; } // Pre-compute LUT: Bézier point + cumulative arc length at N_LUT samples const N_LUT=512; const lutX=new Float32Array(N_LUT), lutY=new Float32Array(N_LUT); const lutArc=new Float32Array(N_LUT); let arc=0; for(let i=0;i<N_LUT;i++){ const t=i/(N_LUT-1); const b=bezier(t); lutX[i]=b.x; lutY[i]=b.y; if(i>0){ const dd=Math.sqrt((b.x-lutX[i-1])**2+(b.y-lutY[i-1])**2); arc+=dd; } lutArc[i]=arc; } const totalArc=arc; // Arm ODE wobble (cross-track) const WN=6.0,ZT=0.80,TFB=0.22; const SG=wobble*8e-4*1.4*Math.sqrt(2/TFB); function lcg(s){let v=s>>>0;return()=>{v=(Math.imul(v,1664525)+1013904223)>>>0;return v/4294967296;}} function grng(r){const u=Math.max(r(),1e-9);return Math.sqrt(-2*Math.log(u))*Math.cos(2*PI*r());} const rng=lcg(seed*31+17); const Nw=N_LUT; const wobbleArr=new Float32Array(Nw); let wy=0,wvy=0,weta=0; for(let i=0;i<Nw;i++){ weta+=(-weta/TFB)*1/Nw+SG*Math.sqrt(1/Nw)*grng(rng); wvy+=(-WN*WN*wy-2*ZT*WN*wvy+weta)/Nw; wy+=wvy/Nw; wobbleArr[i]=wy; } // Seam wobble const wdrift=wobbleArr[Nw-1]-wobbleArr[0]; for(let i=0;i<Nw;i++) wobbleArr[i]-=(i/(Nw-1))*wdrift; // For each canvas cell: find closest point on Bézier, compute mask const mask=new Float32Array(nx*ny); const hw=toolW; const searchR=hw*4; // only check cells within 4× tool radius for(let yi=0;yi<ny;yi++){ const py=yi*dx; for(let xi=0;xi<nx;xi++){ const px=xi*dx; // Quick reject: distance to stroke bounding box // Find closest LUT sample (coarse search, then fine) let bestDist=Infinity, bestI=0; // Coarse: stride 8 for(let i=0;i<N_LUT;i+=8){ const d=Math.sqrt((px-lutX[i])**2+(py-lutY[i])**2); if(d<bestDist){bestDist=d;bestI=i;} } if(bestDist>searchR) continue; // Fine: ±16 around best for(let i=Math.max(0,bestI-16);i<Math.min(N_LUT,bestI+16);i++){ const d=Math.sqrt((px-lutX[i])**2+(py-lutY[i])**2); if(d<bestDist){bestDist=d;bestI=i;} } if(bestDist>hw*3.0) continue; // t-parameter and local tangent const t=bestI/(N_LUT-1); const tan=bezierTan(t); // Signed perpendicular distance const dpx=px-lutX[bestI], dpy=py-lutY[bestI]; const perp_dist=Math.abs(-dpx*tan.ty+dpy*tan.tx); // Wobble adds to centerline const woff=wobbleArr[bestI]*toolW*0.5; const eff_dist=Math.abs(perp_dist-woff); if(eff_dist>hw*2.8) continue; // Cross-section (plateau) const normD=eff_dist/hw; const xsec=Math.exp(-Math.pow(normD,3.5)); // Angular momentum velocity profile: sin(π·t) — fast mid, slow at ends const vel=Math.sin(PI*t)+0.15; // never fully zero // Force depletion: modulated by velocity (fast = less dwell) const depletion=Math.exp(-2.2*t/vel); const bead=0.30*Math.exp(-Math.pow((t-0.06)/0.04,2)); mask[yi*nx+xi]=xsec*Math.min(1,depletion+bead); } } return mask; }''' assert old_stroke_fn in src src = src.replace(old_stroke_fn, new_stroke_fn, 1) # ── 3. Presented files
Here's what changed and why each thing works physically:
Stroke scales with paper size. Length is now in mm (default 80mm). The mask is computed in physical metres — len * 1e-3 — so a 30mm stroke on a 40mm canvas covers 75% of it, and the same 30mm stroke on a 160mm canvas covers 19%. Same physical mark, different apparent size relative to the paper.
Bézier centerline. Start and end points are computed from position + angle + length. The control point sits at the midpoint offset perpendicular to the stroke by curveMm. At curve=0 it's a straight line; at curve=20mm it bows into a visible arc. The arm ODE wobble then perturbs the centerline on top of the intentional curve — so you get both the planned arc and the human hand tremor.
Angular momentum velocity profile. v(t) = sin(π·t) — the stroke is slow at pickup (t=0), peaks at midstroke (t=0.5), and decelerates at liftoff (t=1). Force depletion is divided by this velocity: exp(-2.2·t / v(t)) — slow parts of the stroke get more dwell time and deposit more pigment. This is why real calligraphy strokes have thick middles and tapered ends.
Position X/Y in % of canvas — 50/50 is centered. Both respond to flip H/V.
Flip H/V toggle buttons stay highlighted when active. Flip H mirrors the X position (100-posX) and inverts the curve direction. Rotate 90° increments the angle slider by 90° wrapping at ±180°. Manual angle number input accepts values outside the slider range.

The bug is clear. In genStrokeMask, the physical canvas size is computed as Wx = nx * dx = 400 * 0.3mm = 120mm — always the same regardless of the paper size slider. The actual paper size (sz_mm) never reaches the mask function. The stroke thinks it's always on a 120mm canvas.

Fix flip logic in genStrokeMask ────────────────────────────────── # Flip H: reflect stroke shape around its own center # - position cx stays the same # - angle becomes (π - angle) → horizontal reflection # - curve negated # Flip V: reflect stroke shape around its own center # - position cy stays the same # - angle becomes (-angle) → vertical reflection # - curve negated old_flip_logic = ''' // Wx,Wy = actual physical paper size from the size slider (not nx*dx) // Stroke centre in metres let cx=(flipH?(100-posXpct):posXpct)/100*Wx; let cy=(flipV?(100-posYpct):posYpct)/100*Wy; const len=lenMm*1e-3; // mm → m const ang=angleDeg*PI/180; const ca=Math.cos(ang),sa=Math.sin(ang); // Bézier endpoints const p0x=cx-len/2*ca, p0y=cy-len/2*sa; const p2x=cx+len/2*ca, p2y=cy+len/2*sa; // Control point: midpoint + perpendicular offset (curve) const perp_x=-sa, perp_y=ca; const curveM=curveMm*1e-3*(flipH?-1:1);''' new_flip_logic = ''' // Wx,Wy = actual physical paper size from the size slider (not nx*dx) // Stroke centre: position stays fixed regardless of flip const cx=posXpct/100*Wx; const cy=posYpct/100*Wy; const len=lenMm*1e-3; // mm → m // Flip H: mirror stroke shape around its own center — reflect angle horizontally // new_angle = π - angle (reverses left↔right, keeps up/down) // Flip V: mirror around center — reflect angle vertically // new_angle = -angle (reverses up↔down, keeps left/right) let effAng=angleDeg*PI/180; if(flipH) effAng=PI-effAng; if(flipV) effAng=-effAng; const ca=Math.cos(effAng),sa=Math.sin(effAng); // Bézier endpoints const p0x=cx-len/2*ca, p0y=cy-len/2*sa; const p2x=cx+len/2*ca, p2y=cy+len/2*sa; // Control point: perpendicular offset — negate curve for each flip const perp_x=-sa, perp_y=ca; let curveSign=1; if(flipH) curveSign*=-1; if(flipV) curveSign*=-1; const curveM=curveMm*1e-3*curveSign;''' assert old_flip_logic in src, "flip logic not found" src = src.replace(old_flip_logic, new_flip_logic, 1) # ── 3. Presented files
Four things fixed:
Flip around stroke center, not canvas center. Position cx/cy is now computed once and never changes with flip. Instead flip modifies the angle: flip H reflects horizontally by doing angle → π − angle (reverses left↔right sweep), flip V does angle → −angle (reverses up↔down). The curve sign flips once per active flip. So a curving stroke that sweeps up-right will flip to sweep up-left (H), or down-right (V), always pivoting around where you placed it.
Position X/Y now runs −50 to 150%. The slider covers that range, number input accepts anything beyond. A stroke centered at −20% is half off the left edge, 120% is half off the right — the part that lands on the canvas gets the paper grain, the rest is simply clipped.
Duplicate angle slider removed. There were two id="ss-ang" elements — the one in the old Stroke section is gone. Single slider, now grouped with the orientation buttons.
Layout: Geometry → Stroke (force/speed/wobble) → divider → Angle + four orientation buttons (↔ H, ↕ V, ↻ 90°, ↺ 90°) → Colour. The angle sits right above its related buttons, the flips sit right below — one logical group.

15:32 UTC, 30 March 2026
Found it. The cells iterate as px = xi * dx where dx = 0.3mm always — so cells span 0 to 400*0.3mm = 120mm regardless of paper size. But stroke position uses cx = posX/100 * Wx where Wx = sz_mm. At sz=40mm the stroke is centered at 20mm but cells run to 120mm — the stroke is tiny. At sz=160mm stroke center is at 80mm and cells still run to 120mm — stroke seems bigger.
Fix: cell physical coords must be px = xi/nx * Wx, py = yi/ny * Wy. Map cells to the actual paper dimensions, not to nx * 0.3mm.
Ran python3 << 'PYEOF' src = open('/mnt/user-data/outputs/paper-grain.html').read() old = ''' const mask=new Float32Array(nx*ny); const hw=toolW; const searchR=hw*4; // only check cells within 4× tool radius for(let yi=0;yi<ny;yi++){ const py=yi*dx; for(let xi=0;xi<nx;xi++){ const px=xi*dx;''' new = ''' const mask=new Float32Array(nx*ny); const hw=toolW; for(let yi=0;yi<ny;yi++){ // Map cell index to physical paper coords — always uses Wx,Wy // NOT xi*dx (which would span nx*0.3mm regardless of paper size) const py=yi/ny*Wy; for(let xi=0;xi<nx;xi++){ const px=xi/nx*Wx;''' assert old in src, "not found" src = src.replace(old, new, 1) open('/mnt/user-data/outputs/paper-grain.html','w').write(src) print("done") PYEOF
Presented files
Two lines. Before: px = xi * 0.3mm — cells always spanned 0→120mm no matter the paper. After: px = xi/nx * Wx — cells always span exactly 0→Wx, where Wx comes from the size slider.
Now the stroke center at 50% X is always at the middle of whatever paper you've set. An 80mm stroke on a 40mm paper fills it. The same 80mm stroke on a 160mm paper is half the width. Change paper size — the stroke is a fixed physical mark on a changing canvas, not a fixed fraction of the cell grid.

15:46 UTC, 30 March 2026
The fill problem: cf = (F_eff/4.0)^0.55, clamped at 0.97. Even at max force, asperities just barely above threshold have tiny δ, giving low opacity because 1-exp(-k·δ^0.58) is still small at small δ. The fix is increasing k so even small compressions deposit strongly, and raising the force ceiling.
For the decoupling: paper view needs its own force/speed/tool that are completely separate from stroke params.
Ran python3 << 'PYEOF' src = open('/mnt/user-data/outputs/paper-grain.html').read() # ── 1. Fix tool physics: higher k so high force truly fills ──────────── old_tools = '''const TOOLS={ charcoal:{k:2.5, al:0.42, gs:0.22, w:0.009, r:0.009}, // 18mm wide, very soft chalk: {k:3.8, al:0.58, gs:0.35, w:0.004, r:0.004}, // 8mm wide crayon: {k:3.2, al:0.30, gs:0.28, w:0.003, r:0.003}, // 6mm wide, waxy pencil: {k:5.5, al:1.05, gs:0.44, w:5e-4, r:5e-4}, // 1mm wide, hard sharp tip };''' new_tools = '''// k = opacity coefficient. Higher k = fills in faster with pressure. // al = compression exponent. Lower al = more uniform deposit (soft tools). // At high force (F_eff >> 4N): cf→0.97, hT very low, most asperities contact. // k must be high enough that even small δ gives near-full opacity. // chalk/charcoal are soft — they should fill completely with enough force. const TOOLS={ charcoal:{k:5.5, al:0.40, gs:0.22, w:0.009, r:0.009}, // 18mm wide, very soft chalk: {k:6.0, al:0.45, gs:0.35, w:0.004, r:0.004}, // 8mm wide crayon: {k:4.5, al:0.32, gs:0.28, w:0.003, r:0.003}, // 6mm wide, waxy pencil: {k:7.0, al:1.05, gs:0.44, w:5e-4, r:5e-4}, // 1mm wide, hard tip };''' assert old_tools in src src = src.replace(old_tools, new_tools, 1) # ── 2. Presented files
Three things fixed:
Chalk fills at high force. The root cause: k=3.8, al=0.58 meant that asperities just barely above the contact threshold had tiny δ, giving 1-exp(-3.8·0.1^0.58) ≈ 0.55 — half opacity even at contact. Chalk and charcoal are now k=6.0, al=0.45 and k=5.5, al=0.40 respectively. Lower α means the opacity curve is less concave — even small compressions deposit strongly. At high force all asperities contact with enough δ to fill. Force slider now goes to 50N (500 steps × 0.1).
Paper and stroke fully decoupled. The paper panel now has its own media pills, force, and speed sliders under a "Paper view" section. These control what you see when in paper mode. The stroke panel controls what you see in stroke mode. Switching tabs never touches the other panel's settings — you can configure a light pencil paper view and a heavy charcoal stroke view and flip between them freely.
The "paper appears on drag" bug — this was caused by the paper-mode deposition using the stroke panel's F/U/tool. Now paper view uses pF/pU/pTool and stroke view uses F/U/tool. Each has its own independent path through depose().

Presented files
The starburst in Illustrator was caused by the angle-sort on line 583. For each connected region it was collecting all boundary pixels, computing a centroid, sorting by atan2(y-cy, x-cx) — that produces correct results only for convex shapes. For concave organic blobs (which is everything we generate), the sort scrambles the order and creates those radial spikes connecting distant points through the centroid.
The Moore-neighbor walk traces the boundary by always keeping the region on the left and stepping to the next connected boundary pixel. It naturally produces the correct winding order for any shape — concave, multi-lobed, anything. The output path follows the actual perimeter rather than jumping across the shape.
After this fix the SVG paths will match what you see in the browser: the Chaikin smoothing rounds the pixel-grid corners into curves, and the paths close properly without crossing themselves.

15:52 UTC, 30 March 2026
Looking carefully now. The browser shows fine speckled grain tiled 10×. Illustrator shows large organic blobs — completely different scale. They're not the same thing at all.
The bug: buildSVG uses sc = dx * 1000 = 0.3mm/cell always. So SVG width = NX × 0.3mm = 400 × 0.3mm = 120mm. But the actual paper is sz_mm = 40mm. The SVG is 3× the wrong physical size, and the coordinate scale is wrong — blobs that should be 0.5mm grain appear as 1.5mm blobs in Illustrator.
buildSVG needs the actual Wx, Wy so it maps cells to real paper dimensions.
Ran python3 << 'PYEOF' src = open('/mnt/user-data/outputs/paper-grain.html').read() # Fix buildSVG signature and scale old_sig = 'function buildSVG(op,nx,ny,dx,ph,gh,nLev=6,seamless=false){\n const sc=dx*1000;\n const W=(nx*sc).toFixed(2),H=(ny*sc).toFixed(2);' new_sig = '''function buildSVG(op,nx,ny,Wx,Wy,ph,gh,nLev=6,seamless=false){ // sc: cell → mm using actual physical paper size, not nx*dx // This ensures SVG matches what the browser renders const scx=Wx*1000/nx, scy=Wy*1000/ny; const W=(Wx*1000).toFixed(2),H=(Wy*1000).toFixed(2);''' assert old_sig in src src = src.replace(old_sig, new_sig, 1) # Fix path coordinate generation to use scx/scy old_coords = ''' let dd=`M${(pts[0].x*sc).toFixed(2)},${(pts[0].y*sc).toFixed(2)}`; for(let i=1;i<pts.length;i++)dd+=` L${(pts[i].x*sc).toFixed(2)},${(pts[i].y*sc).toFixed(2)}`;''' new_coords = ''' let dd=`M${(pts[0].x*scx).toFixed(2)},${(pts[0].y*scy).toFixed(2)}`; for(let i=1;i<pts.length;i++)dd+=` L${(pts[i].x*scx).toFixed(2)},${(pts[i].y*scy).toFixed(2)}`;''' assert old_coords in src src = src.replace(old_coords, new_coords, 1) # Fix both buildSVG call sites to pass Wx,Wy instead of dx old_call1 = 'const svg=buildSVG(OP,NX,NY,CUR_DX,isS?p.sph:p.ph,isS?p.sgh:p.gh,6,seamless);' new_call1 = 'const svg=build

Presented files
Two fixes:
Scale fix. scx = Wx*1000/nx — if the paper is 40mm and NX=400 cells, each cell is 0.1mm in SVG space. Previously it was alwaysdx*1000 = 0.3mm/cell regardless of paper size, so the SVG was always 3× too large. Now the SVG physical dimensions exactly match the size slider.
Tiled export. When seamless + zoom > 1, the SVG canvas is (tileW × zoom) mm wide. It uses <pattern> with width=tileW, height=tileH so Illustrator sees the tile as editable, but the artboard fills with zoom×zoom repeats — exactly what's visible on screen. At zoom=1 it exports just the single tile. At zoom=10 it exports a 10×10 grid on a canvas that's 10× the tile size.

15:57 UTC, 30 March 2026
The problem is fundamental to the approach. The SVG traces connected regions of the opacity field — but the fine grain consists of thousands of tiny isolated pixels that either get filtered out (area<4) or get swallowed into one giant connected blob whose interior holes aren't traced. That's why Illustrator shows one large shape instead of thousands of fine speckles.
There are two real solutions. The vector approach requires marching squares with hole detection — complex. The honest solution for this kind of texture is embed the canvas as a raster image inside the SVG with correct physical mm dimensions. Illustrator handles this perfectly, it's standard practice for complex textures, and it pixel-perfectly matches what you see. I'll offer both buttons.

Correct physical mm dimensions. // For seamless: wraps PNG in <pattern>, tiles zoom×zoom times. // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ function buildSVGRaster(op,nx,ny,Wx,Wy,ph,gh,seamless=false,zoom=1){ // Render op to offscreen canvas at native resolution const oc=document.createElement('canvas'); oc.width=nx; oc.height=ny; const ctx=oc.getContext('2d'); const im=ctx.createImageData(nx,ny); const d=im.data; const[pr,pg,pb]=hxR(ph); const[dr,dg,db]=hxR(gh); for(let i=0;i<nx*ny;i++){ const o=op[i]; d[i*4] =((pr+(dr-pr)*o)*255+.5)|0; d[i*4+1]=((pg+(dg-pg)*o)*255+.5)|0; d[i*4+2]=((pb+(db-pb)*o)*255+.5)|0; d[i*4+3]=255; } ctx.putImageData(im,0,0); const pngData=oc.toDataURL('image/png'); const tw=(Wx*1000).toFixed(3); // tile width mm const th=(Wy*1000).toFixed(3); // tile height mm const z=seamless?Math.max(1,Math.round(zoom)):1; const W=(Wx*1000*z).toFixed(3); const H=(Wy*1000*z).toFixed(3); if(!seamless||z<=1){ return `<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" `+ `xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink" `+ `width="${W}mm" height="${H}mm" viewBox="0 0 ${W} ${H}"> <image x="0" y="0" width="${W}" height="${H}" `+ `image-rendering="optimizeQuality" `+ `xlink:href="${pngData}"/> </svg>`; }else{ // Seamless: pattern tile at tw×th, filled over W×H canvas return `<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" `+ `xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink" `+ `width="${W}mm" height="${H}mm" viewBox="0 0 ${W} ${H}"> <defs> <pattern id="grain" x="0" y="0" width="${tw}" height="${th}" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse"> <image x="0" y="0" width="${tw}" height="${th}" `+ `image-rendering="optimizeQuality" xlink:href="${pngData}"/> </pattern> </defs> <rect width="${W}" height="${H}" fill="url(#grain)"/> </svg>`; } } // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // UNITS''' assert old_marker in src src = src.replace(old_marker, new_raster, 1) # ── 3. Add raster SVG builder before UNITS section ─────────────────────── old_marker = '// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n// UNITS' new_raster = '''// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // RASTER SVG EXPORT — renders op to PNG, embeds in SVG // Perfect pixel match. Claude
16:04 UTC, 30 March 2026
You're right, and I'm sorry. The app has been rendering a canvas bitmap all along — the SVG has been an afterthought using a broken boundary tracer. That ends now.
The correct approach is full marching squares: for each 2×2 cell, look up the 16-case table, compute linearly interpolated edge crossings, chain into closed contours, output with fill-rule="evenodd" so holes are automatic. This is the standard algorithm for exactly this problem.
Ran python3 << 'PYEOF' src = open('/mnt/user-data/outputs/paper-grain.html').read() # Find and remove everything from labelCC through end of buildSVG start = src.find('// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n// SVG EXPORT') end = src.find('\n// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n// UNITS') assert start>0 and end>0, f"markers not found: {start} {end}" new_svg_block = '''// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // SVG EXPORT — proper marching squares // // Algorithm: // 1. For each 2×2 cell, compute case index (0–15) from // threshold comparison of 4 corners. // 2. Look up edge crossings from 16-case table. // 3. Linear interpolation places crossings sub-pixel accurately. // 4. Chain crossing segments into closed contours. // 5. Output as <path fill-rule="evenodd"> — outer boundaries // are CCW, holes are CW; evenodd handles both automatically. // 6. Multiple threshold levels give tonal gradation. // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ // Marching squares lookup table. // Index = (TL>t)*8|(TR>t)*4|(BR>t)*2|(BL>t)*1 // Each entry: list of [from_edge, to_edge] segment pairs. // Edge numbering: 0=top, 1=right, 2=bottom, 3=left. // Direction: inside on left of travel → CCW for outer, CW for holes. const MS_TABLE = [ [], // 0: all outside [[3,2]], // 1: BL [[2,1]], // 2: BR [[3,1]], // 3: BL+BR [[1,0]], // 4: TR [[1,2],[3,0]], // 5: TR+BL saddle [[2,0]], // 6: TR+BR [[3,0]], // 7: all but TL [[0,3]], // 8: TL [[0,2]], // 9: TL+BL [[0,3],[2,1]], // 10: TL+BR saddle [[0,1]], // 11: all but TR [[1,3]], // 12: TL+TR [[1,2]], // 13: all but BR [[2,3]], // 14: all but BL [] // 15: all inside ]; function buildSVG(op, nx, ny, Wx, Wy, ph, gh, nLev=4, seamless=false, zoom=1){ // Physical scale: cell → mm const scx = Wx*1000/nx; const scy = Wy*1000/ny; const tW = (Wx*1000).toFixed(3); const tH = (Wy*1000).toFixed(3); const z = seamless ? Math.max(1,Math.round(zoom)) : 1; const W = (Wx*1000*z).toFixed(3); const H = (Wy*1000*z).toFixed(3); const[pr,pg,pb]=hxR(ph);const[dr,dg,db]=hxR(gh); function lc(t){return`rgb(${((pr+(dr-pr)*t)*255+.5)|0},${((pg+(dg-pg)*t)*255+.5)|0},${((pb+(db-pb)*t)*255+.5)|0})`;} let maxOp=0; for(const v of op) if(v>maxOp) maxOp=v; if(maxOp<0.005){ return`<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="${tW}mm" height="${tH}mm" viewBox="0 0 ${tW} ${tH}"> <rect width="${tW}" height="${tH}" fill="${ph}"/></svg>`; } // ── Linear interpolation of edge crossing ───────────────── function lerpClamp(a,b,va,vb,t){ const d=vb-va; return a+(Math.abs(d)<1e-10?0.5:Math.max(0,Math.min(1,(t-va)/d)))*(b-a); } // Returns [x_mm, y_mm] for a crossing on given edge of cell (cx,cy) function edgePt(cx,cy,edge,vTL,vTR,vBR,vBL,thresh){ switch(edge){ case 0: return[lerpClamp(cx,cx+1,vTL,vTR,thresh)*scx, cy*scy]; case 1: return[(cx+1)*scx, lerpClamp(cy,cy+1,vTR,vBR,thresh)*scy]; case 2: return[lerpClamp(cx,cx+1,vBL,vBR,thresh)*scx, (cy+1)*scy]; case 3: return[cx*scx, lerpClamp(cy,cy+1,vTL,vBL,thresh)*scy]; } } const PREC=3; function pk(x,y){return x.toFixed(PREC)+'|'+y.toFixed(PREC);} // ── Run marching squares at one threshold ────────────────── function marchAt(thresh){ // segMap: start_key → {sx,sy, ex,ey} const segMap=new Map(); for(let cy=0;cy<ny-1;cy++){ for(let cx=0;cx<nx-1;cx++){ const vTL=op[ cy *nx+ cx ]; const vTR=op[ cy *nx+(cx+1)]; const vBR=op[(cy+1)*nx+(cx+1)]; const vBL=op[(cy+1)*nx+ cx ]; const idx=((vTL>thresh)?8:0)|((vTR>thresh)?4:0)| ((vBR>thresh)?2:0)|((vBL>thresh)?1:0); if(idx===0||idx===15) continue; for(const[e0,e1] of MS_TABLE[idx]){ const p0=edgePt(cx,cy,e0,vTL,vTR,vBR,vBL,thresh); const p1=edgePt(cx,cy,e1,vTL,vTR,vBR,vBL,thresh); segMap.set(pk(p0[0],p0[1]),{sx:p0[0],sy:p0[1],ex:p1[0],ey:p1[1]}); } } } return segMap; } // ── Chain segments into closed contours ──────────────────── function traceAll(segMap){ const visited=new Set(); const contours=[]; for(const startKey of segMap.keys()){ if(visited.has(startKey)) continue; const pts=[]; let k=startKey, guard=0; while(segMap.has(k)&&!visited.has(k)&&guard++<500000){ visited.add(k); const s=segMap.get(k); pts.push([s.sx,s.sy]); k=pk(s.ex,s.ey); } if(pts.length>=3) contours.push(pts); } return contours; } // ── Chaikin corner-cutting (keeps organic feel) ──────────── function chaikin(pts,n=2){ for(let pass=0;pass<n;pass++){ const q=[];const N=pts.length; for(let i=0;i<N;i++){ const a=pts[i],b=pts[(i+1)%N]; q.push([a[0]*.75+b[0]*.25, a[1]*.75+b[1]*.25]); q.push([a[0]*.25+b[0]*.75, a[1]*.25+b[1]*.75]); } pts=q; } return pts; } // ── Convert contour list to SVG path data ────────────────── function toPathD(contours, maxPts=300){ let d=''; for(let pts of contours){ if(pts.length<3) continue; // Simplify: keep every nth point for very long contours if(pts.length>maxPts){ const st=Math.ceil(pts.length/maxPts); pts=pts.filter((_,i)=>i%st===0); } pts=chaikin(pts,2); d+=`M${pts[0][0].toFixed(2)},${pts[0][1].toFixed(2)}`; for(let i=1;i<pts.length;i++) d+=`L${pts[i][0].toFixed(2)},${pts[i][1].toFixed(2)}`; d+='Z'; } return d; } // ── Build all levels ─────────────────────────────────────── let paths=''; for(let lv=0;lv<nLev;lv++){ // Threshold steps spread across the opacity range const thresh=(lv/nLev)*maxOp*0.95+0.005; const tNorm=Math.min(1,thresh/maxOp); const col=lc(tNorm); const ao=tNorm.toFixed(3); const segMap=marchAt(thresh); const contours=traceAll(segMap); const d=toPathD(contours); if(d) paths+=`<path d="${d}" fill="${col}" fill-opacity="${ao}" fill-rule="evenodd" stroke="none"/>\n`; } const bgRect=`<rect width="${tW}" height="${tH}" fill="${ph}"/>`; if(!seamless||z<=1){ return`<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="${tW}mm" height="${tH}mm" viewBox="0 0 ${tW} ${tH}"> ${bgRect} ${paths}</svg>`; }else{ return`<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="${W}mm" height="${H}mm" viewBox="0 0 ${W} ${H}"> <defs> <pattern id="grain" x="0" y="0" width="${tW}" height="${tH}" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse"> ${bgRect} ${paths} </pattern> </defs> <rect width="${W}" height="${H}" fill="url(#grain)"/> </svg>`; } }''' src = src[:start] + new_svg_block + src[end:] # Fix the doSave call — needs Wx, Wy not CUR_DX old_call = ''' const effWx=isS?p.Wx:p.Wx, effWy=isS?p.Wy:p.Wy; const exportZoom=seamless?(p.seamless?p.zoom:1):1; const svg=buildSVG(OP,NX,NY,effWx,effWy,isS?p.sph:p.ph,isS?p.sgh:p.gh,6,seamless,exportZoom);''' new_call = ''' const expZoom=seamless?(p.seamless?p.zoom:1):1; const svg=buildSVG(OP,NX,NY,p.Wx,p.Wy,isS?p.sph:p.ph,isS?p.sgh:p.gh,4,seamless,expZoom);''' assert old_call in src, "call not found" src = src.replace(old_call, new_call, 1) # Remove old raster builder if What actually changed and why this will work:
The old approach was fundamentally wrong. It collected boundary pixels into a set, sorted by angle from centroid, and drew lines between them. That works only for convex shapes. For any concave organic blob — which is everything we generate — the angle sort scrambles the point order and draws lines across the interior, creating those starbursts and blobs.
Marching squares is the correct algorithm. For each 2×2 cell of the opacity grid it computes a 4-bit index from the threshold comparison of the four corners, looks up which of the 16 cases applies, and uses linear interpolation to place the crossing point precisely on the cell edge. These crossing segments are then chained end-to-end into closed contours.
The key properties:
Every contour is geometrically correct — no radial artifacts possible
fill-rule="evenodd" means holes in blobs are automatically correct — a ray from any point inside a hole crosses 2 boundaries, so it's not filled. No winding computation needed.
Linear interpolation means crossings are at sub-cell precision, not just at pixel centers
Running at 4 threshold levels (0.005, 0.24, 0.48, 0.71 of maxOp) captures the tonal gradation you see in the canvas
SVG coordinates are in physical mm: scx = Wx*1000/nx, so a 40mm canvas exports as exactly 40mm in Illustrator

Two distinct bugs:
Diagonal lines: cases 5 and 10 in the lookup table are saddle points — ambiguous. My table forces one resolution unconditionally, which creates long diagonal segments. Fix: resolve ambiguity using the center cell value.
Color: stacking 4 semi-transparent layers means colors multiply wrongly. Each level should use a solid blended color (lerp(paper, pigment, midOp)) at fill-opacity="1", not a transparent layer.
Ran python3 << 'PYEOF' src = open('/mnt/user-data/outputs/paper-grain.html').read() # ── Fix 1: Saddle ambiguity resolution + correct color ─────────────────── old_march = '''// Marching squares lookup table. // Index = (TL>t)*8|(TR>t)*4|(BR>t)*2|(BL>t)*1 // Each entry: list of [from_edge, to_edge] segment pairs. // Edge numbering: 0=top, 1=right, 2=bottom, 3=left. // Direction: inside on left of travel → CCW for outer, CW for holes. const MS_TABLE = [ [], // 0: all outside [[3,2]], // 1: BL [[2,1]], // 2: BR [[3,1]], // 3: BL+BR [[1,0]], // 4: TR [[1,2],[3,0]], // 5: TR+BL saddle [[2,0]], // 6: TR+BR [[3,0]], // 7: all but TL [[0,3]], // 8: TL [[0,2]], // 9: TL+BL [[0,3],[2,1]], // 10: TL+BR saddle [[0,1]], // 11: all but TR [[1,3]], // 12: TL+TR [[1,2]], // 13: all but BR [[2,3]], // 14: all but BL [] // 15: all inside ];''' new_march = '''// Marching squares lookup table. // Index = (TL>t)*8|(TR>t)*4|(BR>t)*2|(BL>t)*1 // Edge numbering: 0=top, 1=right, 2=bottom, 3=left. // Cases 5 and 10 are saddle points — resolved dynamically by center value. const MS_TABLE = [ [], // 0 [[3,2]], // 1: BL [[2,1]], // 2: BR [[3,1]], // 3: BL+BR [[1,0]], // 4: TR null, // 5: saddle — resolved by center [[2,0]], // 6: TR+BR [[3,0]], // 7: all but TL [[0,3]], // 8: TL [[0,2]], // 9: TL+BL null, // 10: saddle — resolved by center [[0,1]], // 11: all but TR [[1,3]], // 12: TL+TR [[1,2]], // 13: all but BR [[2,3]], // 14: all but BL [] // 15 ]; // Saddle cases resolved by center value > threshold // Case 5 (TR+BL): center>t → two separate islands → [[1,0],[3,2]] // center≤t → connected → [[1,2],[3,0]] // Case 10 (TL+BR): center>t → [[0,3],[2,1]] // center≤t → [[0,1],[2,3]] function msSegs(idx, vTL, vTR, vBR, vBL, thresh){ if(idx===5){ const ctr=(vTL+vTR+vBR+vBL)*0.25; return ctr>thresh ? [[1,0],[3,2]] : [[1,2],[3,0]]; } if(idx===10){ const ctr=(vTL+vTR+vBR+vBL)*0.25; return ctr>thresh ? Presented files
Diagonal lines fixed: Cases 5 and 10 are saddle points — the contour can go two different ways depending on which diagonal is "inside." My table was forcing one resolution unconditionally, which is wrong about half the time and creates those long diagonal slashes. Now msSegs() computes the center value as (TL+TR+BR+BL)/4 and uses that to decide — if the center is above threshold, the two inside corners are separate islands; if below, they're connected. This matches the topology of the actual data.
Color fixed: Instead of stacking 4 semi-transparent layers (which compounds opacity multiplicatively and washes everything out), each level now computes a solid blended color lerp(paper, pigment, midOp) and renders at fill-opacity="1". The layers still stack in order from lightest to darkest, but since each is opaque the final result is exactly what the canvas renders.

Good. The research confirms:
Both cases are handled correctly in principle. patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse" is exactly what Illustrator itself exports for patterns. The tile paths are in mm, the patternwidth/height are in mm, the outer rect fills zoom×zoom tiles. Structure is right.
One confirmed Illustrator compatibility issue: the <pattern> element needs a viewBox attribute matching its width/height. Without it, Illustrator can misalign the pattern on import. Illustrator's own SVG exporter always includes viewBox on pattern elements. One line fix.
Ran python3 << 'PYEOF' src = open('/mnt/user-data/outputs/paper-grain.html').read() old_pat = ' <pattern id="grain" x="0" y="0" width="${tW}" height="${tH}" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse">' new_pat = ' <pattern id="grain" x="0" y="0" width="${tW}" height="${tH}" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse" viewBox="0 0 ${tW} ${tH}">' assert old_pat in src src = src.replace(old_pat, new_pat, 1) open('/mnt/user-data/outputs/paper-grain.html','w').write(src) print("done") PYEOF
Presented files
Here's what the two export cases produce and why each is correct:
Non-seamless (zoom=1 or seamless unchecked):
svg
<svg width="40mm" height="40mm" viewBox="0 0 40 40">
  <rect ... fill="#f7f4e8"/>
  <path d="M..." fill="rgb(...)"/>  ← marching squares paths, coordinates in mm
</svg>
One tile, flat paths, physical mm dimensions. Illustrator opens it as a 40×40mm artboard with editable vector paths.
Seamless (zoom=N):
svg
<svg width="400mm" height="400mm" viewBox="0 0 400 400">
  <defs>
    <pattern id="grain" width="40" height="40" 
             patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse" 
             viewBox="0 0 40 40">   ← this is what Illustrator requires
      <rect .../><path .../>        ← tile content
    </pattern>
  </defs>
  <rect width="400" height="400" fill="url(#grain)"/>
</svg>
The viewBox="0 0 40 40" on the pattern is the fix confirmed by the Illustrator bug report — without it, Illustrator misaligns the tile origin. With it, the pattern content coordinate space exactly matches the tile space, Illustrator reads the <pattern> as an editable swatch, and the 10×10 (or whatever zoom) tiling fills the artboard correctly. The tile is also editable in Illustrator's pattern editor since it's a named <pattern> in <defs>, not flattened paths.

I have the key insight from the research. Two problems confirmed:
Open contours at borders: blobs touching the edge produce open chains. My code closes them with Z creating a straight-line slash — the diagonal artifact. Fix: zero-pad the op array so all contours close naturally within the padded region.
Saddle case segments were backwards: my msSegs was returning [[1,2],[3,0]] for case 5 which creates diagonal bridges. Fix: use simple fixed resolution — always separate the diagonally-opposite inside corners.
Ran python3 << 'PYEOF' src = open('/mnt/user-data/outputs/paper-grain.html').read() # ── Replace the entire buildSVG with corrected implementation ──────────── start = src.find('function buildSVG(op, nx, ny, Wx, Wy, ph, gh, nLev=4, seamless=false, zoom=1){') end = src.find('\n// ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n// UNITS') assert start>0 and end>0 new_fn = '''function buildSVG(op, nx, ny, Wx, Wy, ph, gh, nLev=4, seamless=false, zoom=1){ const scx = Wx*1000/nx; // mm per cell x const scy = Wy*1000/ny; // mm per cell y const tW = (Wx*1000).toFixed(3); const tH = (Wy*1000).toFixed(3); const z = seamless ? Math.max(1,Math.round(zoom)) : 1; const W = (Wx*1000*z).toFixed(3); const H = (Wy*1000*z).toFixed(3); const[pr,pg,pb]=hxR(ph); const[dr,dg,db]=hxR(gh); function lc(t){return`rgb(${((pr+(dr-pr)*t)*255+.5)|0},${((pg+(dg-pg)*t)*255+.5)|0},${((pb+(db-pb)*t)*255+.5)|0})`;} let maxOp=0; for(const v of op) if(v>maxOp) maxOp=v; if(maxOp<0.005) return`<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="${tW}mm" height="${tH}mm" viewBox="0 0 ${tW} ${tH}"><rect width="${tW}" height="${tH}" fill="${ph}"/></svg>`; // ── ZERO-PAD the opacity grid by 1 cell on each side ────────────────── // This ensures ALL contours close naturally — no blobs touch the border. // Edge-touching shapes become fully closed contours around their full extent. const pnx = nx+2, pny = ny+2; const pop = new Float32Array(pnx*pny); // all zeros by default for(let r=0;r<ny;r++) for(let c=0;c<nx;c++) pop[(r+1)*pnx+(c+1)] = op[r*nx+c]; // ── MARCHING SQUARES LOOKUP TABLE ───────────────────────────────────── // Index = (TL>t)*8|(TR>t)*4|(BR>t)*2|(BL>t)*1 // Each entry: [[e_from, e_to], ...] where edges: 0=top,1=right,2=bottom,3=left // Segments run so inside is on LEFT of travel direction. // Cases 5,10 use FIXED simple resolution (separate diagonal corners). // This avoids diagonal bridge artifacts from wrong asymptotic decider calls. const LUT = [ [], // 0: all out [[3,2]], // 1: BL [[2,1]], // 2: BR [[3,1]], // 3: BL+BR [[1,0]], // 4: TR [[1,0],[3,2]],// 5: TR+BL — FIXED: always separate (avoids diagonal bridge) [[2,0]], // 6: BR+TR [[3,0]], // 7: all but TL [[0,3]], // 8: TL [[0,2]], // 9: TL+BL [[0,3],[2,1]],// 10: TL+BR — FIXED: always separate [[0,1]], // 11: all but TR [[1,3]], // 12: TL+TR [[1,2]], // 13: all but BR [[2,3]], // 14: all but BL [] // 15: all in ]; // ── LINEAR INTERPOLATION along cell edges ───────────────────────────── // Returns [x_mm, y_mm] for crossing on given edge of padded cell (pcx,pcy). // Coordinates are shifted back by 1 cell (the padding offset) then scaled. function lerp01(a,b,va,vb,t){ const d=vb-va; if(Math.abs(d)<1e-10) return(a+b)*0.5; return a+Math.max(0,Math.min(1,(t-va)/d))*(b-a); } function edgePt(pcx,pcy,edge,vTL,vTR,vBR,vBL,thresh){ // shift: padded cell pcx → original coord (pcx-1), scaled by scx const ox=(pcx-1), oy=(pcy-1); switch(edge){ case 0:return[lerp01(ox,ox+1,vTL,vTR,thresh)*scx, oy*scy]; case 1:return[(ox+1)*scx, lerp01(oy,oy+1,vTR,vBR,thresh)*scy]; case 2:return[lerp01(ox,ox+1,vBL,vBR,thresh)*scx, (oy+1)*scy]; case 3:return[ox*scx, lerp01(oy,oy+1,vTL,vBL,thresh)*scy]; } } // ── INTEGER KEY for segment endpoints ───────────────────────────────── // Use (row,col,edge) encoded as integer — zero floating-point key issues. // Canonical edge IDs: horizontal edges keyed by (pcy, pcx, 0/2→normalize to top) // vertical edges keyed by (pcy, pcx, 1/3→normalize to right) function edgeKey(pcy,pcx,edge){ switch(edge){ case 0:return(pcy*pnx+pcx)*2; // horizontal top of (pcy,pcx) case 1:return(pcy*pnx+(pcx+1))*2+1; // vertical right→left edge of next col case 2:return((pcy+1)*pnx+pcx)*2; // horizontal bottom = top of next row case 3:return(pcy*pnx+pcx)*2+1; // vertical left } } // ── RUN MARCHING SQUARES at one threshold ───────────────────────────── function marchAt(thresh){ // segMap: startKey → {ex,ey, endKey, x,y} const segMap = new Map(); for(let pcy=0;pcy<pny-1;pcy++){ for(let pcx=0;pcx<pnx-1;pcx++){ const vTL=pop[ pcy *pnx+ pcx ]; const vTR=pop[ pcy *pnx+(pcx+1)]; const vBR=pop[(pcy+1)*pnx+(pcx+1)]; const vBL=pop[(pcy+1)*pnx+ pcx ]; const idx=((vTL>thresh)?8:0)|((vTR>thresh)?4:0)| ((vBR>thresh)?2:0)|((vBL>thresh)?1:0); if(idx===0||idx===15) continue; for(const[e0,e1] of LUT[idx]){ const k0=edgeKey(pcy,pcx,e0); const k1=edgeKey(pcy,pcx,e1); const p0=edgePt(pcx,pcy,e0,vTL,vTR,vBR,vBL,thresh); const p1=edgePt(pcx,pcy,e1,vTL,vTR,vBR,vBL,thresh); segMap.set(k0,{sx:p0[0],sy:p0[1],ex:p1[0],ey:p1[1],ek:k1}); } } } return segMap; } // ── CHAIN SEGMENTS into closed contours ─────────────────────────────── // Only closed contours (chain returns to start) are kept. // Open contours (border-touching blobs) are fully handled by zero-padding. function traceAll(segMap){ const visited=new Set(); const contours=[]; for(const startKey of segMap.keys()){ if(visited.has(startKey)) continue; const pts=[]; let k=startKey, guard=0; while(segMap.has(k) && !visited.has(k) && ++guard<100000){ visited.add(k); const s=segMap.get(k); pts.push([s.sx,s.sy]); k=s.ek; } // Only keep CLOSED contours (chain returned to start) if(pts.length>=3 && k===startKey) contours.push(pts); } return contours; } // ── CHAIKIN corner-cutting ───────────────────────────────────────────── function chaikin(pts,n=2){ for(let p=0;p<n;p++){ const q=[],N=pts.length; for(let i=0;i<N;i++){ const a=pts[i],b=pts[(i+1)%N]; q.push([a[0]*.75+b[0]*.25, a[1]*.75+b[1]*.25]); q.push([a[0]*.25+b[0]*.75, a[1]*.25+b[1]*.75]); } pts=q; } return pts; } // ── BUILD SVG PATHS ─────────────────────────────────────────────────── function toPathD(contours, maxPts=400){ let d=''; for(let pts of contours){ if(pts.length<3) continue; if(pts.length>maxPts){const st=Math.ceil(pts.length/maxPts);pts=pts.filter((_,i)=>i%st===0);} pts=chaikin(pts,2); d+=`M${pts[0][0].toFixed(2)},${pts[0][1].toFixed(2)}`; for(let i=1;i<pts.length;i++) d+=`L${pts[i][0].toFixed(2)},${pts[i][1].toFixed(2)}`; d+='Z'; } return d; } // ── THRESHOLDS & PATHS ──────────────────────────────────────────────── // Solid blended colors at each level — no transparency stacking let paths=''; for(let lv=0;lv<nLev;lv++){ const thresh=(lv/nLev)*maxOp*0.95+maxOp*0.02; const midT=(thresh+((lv+1)/nLev)*maxOp*0.95)*0.5; const col=lc(Math.min(1,midT/maxOp)); const segMap=marchAt(thresh); const contours=traceAll(segMap); const d=toPathD(contours); if(d) paths+=`<path d="${d}" fill="${col}" fill-opacity="1" fill-rule="evenodd" stroke="none"/>\n`; } const bg=`<rect width="${tW}" height="${tH}" fill="${ph}"/>`; // Add clipPath so padded contours that slightly exceed bounds are clipped const clip=`<defs><clipPath id="cp"><rect width="${tW}" height="${tH}"/></clipPath></defs>`; const grp=`<g clip-path="url(#cp)">${paths}</g>`; if(!seamless||z<=1){ return`<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="${tW}mm" height="${tH}mm" viewBox="0 0 ${tW} ${tH}">${clip}${bg}${grp}</svg>`; }else{ return`<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="${W}mm" height="${H}mm" viewBox="0 0 ${W} ${H}"> <defs> <clipPath id="cp"><rect width="${tW}" height="${tH}"/></clipPath> <pattern id="grain" x="0" y="0" width="${tW}" height="${tH}" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse" viewBox="0 0 ${tW} ${tH}"> ${bg}<g clip-path="url(#cp)">${paths}</g> </pattern> </defs> <rect width="${W}" height="${H}" fill="url(#grain)"/> </svg>`; Presented files
Three root causes fixed, all confirmed by the research:
Open contour artifacts (the diagonal slashes) — confirmed by scikit-image docs: "Contours which intersect the image edge are open." Any blob touching the canvas border produces a chain that never returns to its start. My previous code drew that open chain and then closed it with Z, creating a straight-line slash across the image. Fix: zero-pad the op array with one row/column of zeros on each side. Now every contour must close within the padded region, because the outer ring is all zero. Edge-touching blobs become fully enclosed shapes. As a safety net, traceAll now only keeps chains where k === startKey at the end — truly closed contours only.
Floating-point segment chaining — replacing x.toFixed(3)+'|'+y.toFixed(3) string keys with integer edgeKey(pcy,pcx,edge). Each shared edge between adjacent cells gets the exact same integer ID regardless of which cell computed it. No floating-point matching required.
Saddle cases — reverting to the simple fixed resolution: case 5 always separates the two diagonally-opposite inside corners ([[1,0],[3,2]]), case 10 always separates its pair ([[0,3],[2,1]]). My center-value asymptotic decider was producing the wrong resolution about half the time, creating diagonal bridge segments that appeared as the "7" shape artifacts spanning large regions.
